# Define the list of FAANG ticker symbols
faang_symbols_list = ("AMZN", "AAPL", "GOOGL", "META", "NFLX")

# Filter customers who have a .com email address and have made transactions in FAANG stocks.
# - ENDSWITH(email, ".com") checks if the customer's email ends with ".com".
# - HAS(transactions_made.WHERE(ISIN(ticker.symbol, faang_symbols_list))) == 1 checks if the customer
#   has at least one transaction where the ticker symbol is in the faang_symbols_list.
#   - transactions_made is the sub-collection of transactions for each customer.
#   - ticker.symbol accesses the symbol of the ticker associated with a transaction.
#   - ISIN(ticker.symbol, faang_symbols_list) filters transactions to include only FAANG stocks.
selected_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    ENDSWITH(email, ".com") &
    (HAS(transactions_made.WHERE(ISIN(ticker.symbol, faang_symbols_list))) == 1)
)

# Calculate the total count of such distinct customers.
# The Broker collection is used as the top-level context for this global aggregation.
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_distinct_faang_customers_with_com_email = COUNT(selected_customers)
)

# Step 1: Filter customers who are 'Active' and joined on or after January 1, 2023.
# 'sbCustStatus' is used for the customer's status.
# 'sbCustJoinDate' is used for the customer's join date.
active_and_recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (sbCustStatus == "Active") & (sbCustJoinDate >= "2023-01-01")
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered customers by their country ('sbCustCountry')
# and then calculate the count of customers for each country.
# The 'name' parameter in PARTITION gives a conceptual name to the groups.
# The 'by' parameter specifies the field to partition on, which is 'sbCustCountry'.
# In the CALCULATE step:
#   - 'country=sbCustCountry' selects the country from the partition key.
#   - 'tac_count=COUNT(Customers)' counts the number of customers within each country group.
result = active_and_recent_customers.PARTITION(
    name="country_group", by=(sbCustCountry)
).CALCULATE(
    country=sbCustCountry,
    tac_count=COUNT(Customers)
)

[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by claude:
# Filter transactions for the last 8 weeks excluding current week
transactions_last_8_weeks = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME('now')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 8)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_id=transaction_id,
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=(DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 1) | (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 7)  # Saturday=7, Sunday=1
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = transactions_last_8_weeks.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions for stocks and calculate initial date-related attributes.
stock_transactions_base = Transactions.WHERE(ticker.ticker_type == 'stock').CALCULATE(
    original_date_time = date_time,
    transaction_week_start_date = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'), # Determine the start of the week for each transaction
    transaction_day_name = DAYNAME(date_time) # Get the name of the day (e.g., "Monday")
)

# Step 2: Calculate how many weeks ago each transaction's week was, 
# flag weekend transactions, and filter for the last 8 weeks (excluding the current one).
relevant_transactions = stock_transactions_base.CALCULATE(
    week_start_for_txn = transaction_week_start_date, # Carry over for partitioning
    # Calculate the number of weeks between the transaction's week start and the current week's start.
    # An offset of 0 would be the current week, 1 would be last week, etc.
    weeks_ago_offset = DATEDIFF("weeks", transaction_week_start_date, DATETIME('now', 'start of week')),
    # Create a flag (1 for weekend, 0 for weekday)
    is_weekend_indicator = IFF(ISIN(transaction_day_name, ("Saturday", "Sunday")), 1, 0)
).WHERE(
    (weeks_ago_offset >= 1) & (weeks_ago_offset <= 8) # Filter for weeks with offset 1 (last week) to 8 (8 weeks ago)
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered transactions by the start date of their week 
# and calculate total and weekend transaction counts for each week.
weekly_stock_report = relevant_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="transactions_per_week", by=(week_start_for_txn) # Group by the week's start date
).CALCULATE(
    week_starting = week_start_for_txn, # The unique identifier for the week (its start date)
    total_stock_transactions_in_week = COUNT(Transactions), # Total stock transactions in this week
    weekend_stock_transactions_in_week = SUM(Transactions.is_weekend_indicator) # Sum of weekend flags to count weekend transactions
).ORDER_BY(week_starting.DESC()) # Order from most recent week to oldest among the 8 weeks

result = weekly_stock_report

[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current week and the start of the 8-week target period.
# These values are calculated once at the Broker level and made available downstream.
weekly_stock_transaction_summary = Broker.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    target_period_start_val = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-8 weeks')
).Transactions.WHERE(
    # Filter for stock transactions
    (ticker.ticker_type == 'stock') &
    # Filter for transactions within the last 8 weeks, excluding the current week
    (date_time >= target_period_start_val) &
    (date_time < current_week_start_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # Determine the starting date of the week for each transaction for grouping
    transaction_week_label = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    # Flag if the transaction occurred on a weekend (Saturday or Sunday)
    is_on_weekend = ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
).PARTITION(
    # Group transactions by the week they occurred in
    name="weekly_groups", by=(transaction_week_label)
).CALCULATE(
    # Output the week identifier (start date of the week)
    week_starting_on = transaction_week_label,
    # Count total stock transactions for this week
    total_stock_txns = COUNT(Transactions),
    # Count stock transactions that occurred on a weekend for this week
    weekend_stock_txns = SUM(Transactions.is_on_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_starting_on.ASC())

# Filter transactions for the last 8 weeks excluding current week
transactions_last_8_weeks = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now", "start of week")) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now", "start of week")) <= 8)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_id=transaction_id,
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, "start of week"),
    day_of_week=DAYOFWEEK(date_time),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(date_time), (1, 7))  # Saturday=7, Sunday=1
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = transactions_last_8_weeks.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

# Step 1: Calculate necessary information for each transaction from the Broker's Transactions collection
transactions_with_info = Broker.Transactions.CALCULATE(
    transaction_datetime_col = date_time,
    ticker_type_col = ticker.ticker_type,  # Access ticker_type from the related Ticker
    # Calculate how many full weeks ago this transaction occurred relative to the start of the current week.
    # A value of 1 means last week, 2 means the week before last, etc.
    weeks_ago_calculated = DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME('now', 'start of week')),
    # Flag for weekend transactions (1 if weekend, 0 if weekday)
    is_weekend_calculated = IFF(ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday")), 1, 0)
)

# Step 2: Filter for stock transactions in the last 8 weeks (excluding the current week)
filtered_relevant_transactions = transactions_with_info.WHERE(
    (ticker_type_col == 'stock') &
    (weeks_ago_calculated >= 1) &  # Excludes current week (weeks_ago_calculated = 0)
    (weeks_ago_calculated <= 8)   # Includes up to 8 weeks ago
)

# Step 3: Group by week and calculate the required counts
# The PARTITION function groups the filtered_relevant_transactions by the weeks_ago_calculated value.
# For each group (each week), we then calculate the total and weekend transaction counts.
weekly_stock_transaction_counts = filtered_relevant_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_group", by=(weeks_ago_calculated)  # Partition by the calculated 'weeks_ago' value
).CALCULATE(
    week_past_identifier = weeks_ago_calculated,  # This is the 'Nth week ago' identifier (1-8)
    total_stock_txns = COUNT(Transactions),      # Counts all stock transactions in this weekly group
    weekend_stock_txns = SUM(is_weekend_calculated) # Sums the is_weekend_calculated flag (0 or 1) for transactions in this group
).ORDER_BY(week_past_identifier.ASC())  # Order results from most recent week (1) to oldest (8)

result = weekly_stock_transaction_counts

# Define date boundaries for "last week (exclusive of the current week)"
# Assuming 'start of week' in DATETIME function respects Sunday as the start,
# as per the problem statement "The week starts on Sunday".

# _current_week_start_dt represents Sunday 00:00:00 of the current week.
_current_week_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')

# _last_week_start_dt_inclusive represents Sunday 00:00:00 of the previous week.
_last_week_start_dt_inclusive = DATETIME(_current_week_start_dt, '-7 days')

# _last_week_end_dt_exclusive represents Sunday 00:00:00 of the current week.
# Transactions from last week must be strictly before this timestamp.
_last_week_end_dt_exclusive = _current_week_start_dt

# Filter transactions to include only those made by customers from the USA within the defined "last week" period.
_filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (customer.country == "USA") &
    (date_time >= _last_week_start_dt_inclusive) &
    (date_time < _last_week_end_dt_exclusive)
)

# Calculate the number of transactions and the total transaction amount.
# The result is a single record from the Broker collection.
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions = COUNT(_filtered_transactions),
    total_transaction_amount = DEFAULT_TO(SUM(_filtered_transactions.amount), 0.0)
)

# Calculate the number of transactions and total transaction amount for US customers last week.
# "Last week" is defined as Sunday to Saturday of the previous week.
# "The week starts on Sunday" implies DATETIME('start of week') will yield a Sunday.
transactions_summary_last_week_usa = Broker.CALCULATE(
    # var_start_of_current_week is Sunday 00:00:00 of the current week.
    var_start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    # var_start_of_last_week is Sunday 00:00:00 of the previous week.
    var_start_of_last_week = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-7 days')
).CALCULATE(
    # Identify transactions made by US customers within the "last week" date range.
    # Transactions.customer.country accesses the country of the customer who made the transaction.
    # The date range is [var_start_of_last_week, var_start_of_current_week).
    last_week_usa_filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
        (customer.country == "USA") &
        (date_time >= var_start_of_last_week) &
        (date_time < var_start_of_current_week)
    )
).CALCULATE(
    # Count the number of filtered transactions.
    num_transactions = COUNT(last_week_usa_filtered_transactions),
    # Sum the transaction amounts for the filtered transactions.
    # Assuming 'amount' is non-null; SUM typically ignores nulls if any.
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(last_week_usa_filtered_transactions.amount)
)

# Step 1: Define date boundaries at the Broker level.
# These calculated fields will be available to operations on sub-collections of Broker.
# 'current_week_start_dt' will be the Sunday of the current week.
# 'last_week_start_dt' will be the Sunday of the previous week.
# We assume DATETIME('now', 'start of week') correctly uses Sunday as the start of the week
# based on the problem statement and typical system configurations.
broker_context_with_dates = Broker.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    last_week_start_dt = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-7 days')
)

# Step 2: Define the set of relevant transactions.
# This is a "contextless expression" representing transactions made by US customers last week.
# It uses the date boundaries calculated in 'broker_context_with_dates'.
# 'Transactions.customer.country' accesses the country of the customer who made the transaction.
# 'last_week_start_dt' and 'current_week_start_dt' are in scope from 'broker_context_with_dates'.
relevant_transactions_from_usa_last_week = broker_context_with_dates.Transactions.WHERE(
    (customer.country == "USA") &
    (date_time >= last_week_start_dt) &
    (date_time < current_week_start_dt)
)

# Step 3: Aggregate results from this filtered set of transactions.
# This final calculation is performed at the Broker level, providing a single summary row.
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions = COUNT(relevant_transactions_from_usa_last_week),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(relevant_transactions_from_usa_last_week.amount)
)

# Step 1: Filter transactions for "buy" type and within the last 10 days,
# and calculate the ticker symbol for each.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") &
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-10 days')) # Transactions from the last 10 days up to the current moment
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_ticker_symbol = ticker.symbol # Accessing symbol from the related Tickers collection
)

# Step 2: Group the filtered transactions by ticker symbol and count the number of buy transactions for each symbol.
symbol_counts = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="transactions_by_symbol", by=(transaction_ticker_symbol)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = transaction_ticker_symbol, # The ticker symbol from the partition key
    num_buy_transactions = COUNT(Transactions) # Count of buy transactions for this symbol
)

# Step 3: Select the top 2 ticker symbols based on the number of buy transactions.
top_2_bought_symbols = symbol_counts.TOP_K(2, by=num_buy_transactions.DESC())

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only "buy" transactions in the past 10 days,
# and calculate the ticker symbol for partitioning.
# DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) < 10 ensures we get transactions
# from 0 full days ago (today) up to 9 full days ago.
recent_buy_transactions_with_symbol = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) < 10)
).CALCULATE(
    # Extract the ticker symbol from the related Ticker record.
    # 'ticker_sym' is an intermediate variable name for the symbol.
    ticker_sym = ticker.symbol
)

# Step 2: Group the filtered transactions by ticker symbol and count the number of buy transactions for each.
buy_counts_per_ticker = recent_buy_transactions_with_symbol.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", by=(ticker_sym)  # Group by the extracted ticker symbol
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker_sym,  # Output field for the ticker symbol
    num_buy_transactions = COUNT(Transactions)  # Count transactions in each group
)

# Step 3: Select the top 2 ticker symbols based on the number of buy transactions, in descending order.
top_2_bought_tickers = buy_counts_per_ticker.TOP_K(
    2, by=num_buy_transactions.DESC()
)

# The final result containing the ticker symbol and number of buy transactions for the top 2.
result = top_2_bought_tickers

[DEBUG] [Q7] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions for "buy" type and within the last 10 days (today + 9 previous days),
# and calculate the ticker symbol for partitioning.
filtered_buy_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) < 10) # Covers 10 days: 0 (today) to 9 days ago
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_val=ticker.symbol # Extract ticker symbol for grouping
    # transaction_id is implicitly available for COUNT(Transactions) to work on distinct transactions
)

# Step 2: Group by ticker symbol and count the number of buy transactions in each group.
# 'Transactions' in COUNT(Transactions) refers to the records from the 'Transactions' collection
# that satisfy the conditions of 'filtered_buy_transactions' and belong to the current partition group.
ticker_transaction_counts = filtered_buy_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", by=(ticker_symbol_val)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_s=ticker_symbol_val, # The ticker symbol from the partition key
    num_buys=COUNT(Transactions) # Count of buy transactions for this ticker symbol
)

# Step 3: Select the top 2 ticker symbols based on the number of buy transactions.
top_2_frequent_tickers = ticker_transaction_counts.TOP_K(
    2, by=num_buys.DESC()
)

# Step 4: Format the final output to return the ticker symbol and the count of buy transactions.
result = top_2_frequent_tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_s,
    number_of_buy_transactions=num_buys
)

[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by claude:
# Filter customers who joined in the last 6 months excluding current month
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("months", join_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 6 &
    DATEDIFF("months", join_date, DATETIME('now')) > 1
).CALCULATE(
    join_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0")),
    customer_id=_id
)

# Group by month to calculate PMCS and PMAT
result = recent_customers.PARTITION(name="months", by=join_month).CALCULATE(
    month=join_month,
    PMCS=COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current month and the start of the 6-month window
date_info = Broker.CALCULATE(
    current_month_start_dt=DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    window_start_dt=DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of month'), '-6 months')
)

# Process customers: calculate their signup month string and pass down date boundaries
customers_processed = date_info.Customers.CALCULATE(
    _id=_id, # Keep _id for counting purposes
    join_date=join_date, # Needed for filtering
    signup_month_str=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0")),
    # Pass through date boundaries from the parent context
    current_month_start_dt=current_month_start_dt,
    window_start_dt=window_start_dt,
    # Keep transactions_made for PMAT calculation
    transactions_made=transactions_made
)

# Filter customers who signed up in the last 6 months, excluding the current month
customers_in_window = customers_processed.WHERE(
    (join_date >= window_start_dt) & (join_date < current_month_start_dt)
)

# Partition by the signup month string and calculate PMCS and PMAT
monthly_metrics = customers_in_window.PARTITION(name="monthly_signup_group", by=(signup_month_str)).CALCULATE(
    month_col=signup_month_str,
    PMCS=COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
)

# Order the results by month
result = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(month_col.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Define the time window boundaries using the Broker collection.
# 'current_month_start_date' is the first day of the current month.
# 'six_months_ago_start_date' is the first day of the month, 6 months before the current month.
time_boundaries = Broker.CALCULATE(
    current_month_start_date=DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    six_months_ago_start_date=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
)

# Step 2 & 3: Filter customers who joined within the defined time window
# (join_date >= six_months_ago_start_date AND join_date < current_month_start_date)
# and create a formatted string for their join month (e.g., "2023-04").
customers_in_period = time_boundaries.Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= six_months_ago_start_date) & (join_date < current_month_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    # Format the join month as YYYY-MM. LPAD ensures month is two digits (e.g., '04' for April).
    cust_join_year_month_str=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 4: Partition the filtered customers by their signup month string.
# This groups customers based on the month they signed up.
monthly_signups_data = customers_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_group", by=(cust_join_year_month_str)
).CALCULATE(
    # Step 5a: Calculate PMCS (Per Month Customer Signups)
    # This is the count of customers within each monthly signup group.
    month_col=cust_join_year_month_str,
    PMCS_val=COUNT(Customers),
    # Step 5b: Calculate PMAT (Per Month Average Transaction amount)
    # This is the average 'amount' of all transactions made by customers who signed up in this specific month.
    PMAT_val=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
)

# Step 6: Select the final columns with the required names and order by month.
result = monthly_signups_data.CALCULATE(
    month=month_col,
    PMCS=PMCS_val,
    PMAT=PMAT_val
).ORDER_BY(month_col.ASC())

# Step 1 & 2: Filter customers who joined in 2022 and calculate necessary flags.
# 'customer_country_field' is used for the country to partition by.
# 'has_transactions_flag' checks if the customer has any transactions.
# 'is_active_flag' checks if the customer's status is 'active'.
customers_base_info = Customers.WHERE(YEAR(join_date) == 2022).CALCULATE(
    customer_country_field=country,
    has_transactions_flag=(HAS(transactions_made) == 1),
    is_active_flag=(status == 'active')
)

# Step 3 & 4: Partition by country and calculate the components for AR.
# 'country_partition_group' is the name of the partition.
# Within each country group, we count relevant customers from the 'customers_base_info' collection.
country_level_stats = customers_base_info.PARTITION(name="country_partition_group", by=(customer_country_field)).CALCULATE(
    # 'output_country_name' will hold the country from the partition key.
    output_country_name=customer_country_field,

    # Numerator for AR: Count of customers (joined in 2022, in this country) 
    # who are active AND have transactions.
    # 'Customers' here refers to the records from 'customers_base_info' within the current partition.
    num_active_customers_with_transactions=COUNT(Customers.WHERE(is_active_flag & has_transactions_flag)),

    # Denominator for AR: Count of customers (joined in 2022, in this country) 
    # who have transactions.
    total_customers_with_transactions=COUNT(Customers.WHERE(has_transactions_flag))
)

# Step 5 & 6: Calculate the final Activity Ratio (AR) for each country.
# The result will include the country and its AR.
# We handle division by zero by setting AR to 0.0 if there are no customers with transactions.
final_ar_by_country = country_level_stats.CALCULATE(
    country=output_country_name,
    AR=IFF(
        total_customers_with_transactions > 0,
        (num_active_customers_with_transactions * 100.0) / total_customers_with_transactions,
        0.0 
    )
)

    name="country_group",  # Name for the partition group
    by=(cust_country)      # Partition by the aliased country
).CALCULATE(

    num_active_customers_with_tx=SUM(is_active_customer_flag),  # Sum of active flags within the partition
    total_customers_with_tx=COUNT(),  # Count of customers within the partition (all have transactions)

    AR_calc=IFF(

        (SUM(is_active_customer_flag) * 100.0) / COUNT(),
        0.0 # Default AR to 0 if no customers with transactions in the group
    )

[DEBUG] [Q10] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter customers who joined in 2022 and have at least one transaction.
# These are the customers relevant for the AR calculation.
relevant_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (YEAR(join_date) == 2022) & (HAS(transactions_made) == 1)
)

# Step 2: For these relevant customers, get their country and determine if their status is 'active'.
customer_info_for_ar = relevant_customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_country_field=country,
    is_customer_active_status=(status == 'active') # True if status is 'active', False otherwise
)

# Step 3: Partition these customers by their country.
country_partitioned_data = customer_info_for_ar.PARTITION(
    name="country_group",  # Name for the collection of groups
    by=(customer_country_field)  # Partition key
)

# Step 4: Calculate AR for each country.
# Within each partition (country_group):
# - 'Customers' refers to the subset of 'customer_info_for_ar' for that specific country.
# - COUNT(Customers) gives the total number of customers with transactions in that country (denominator).
# - SUM(IFF(Customers.is_customer_active_status == True, 1, 0)) counts active customers with transactions (numerator).
ar_by_country = country_partitioned_data.CALCULATE(
    output_country_name=customer_country_field,
    # Denominator: Total customers in this country group (who joined in 2022 and have transactions)
    total_cust_with_trans_in_country=COUNT(Customers),
    # Numerator: Active customers in this country group (who joined in 2022 and have transactions)
    active_cust_with_trans_in_country=SUM(IFF(Customers.is_customer_active_status == True, 1, 0)),
    # Calculate Activity Ratio (AR)
    # AR = (Numerator / Denominator) * 100
    # Ensure no division by zero.
    calculated_activity_ratio=IFF(
        COUNT(Customers) > 0, # Check if denominator is greater than 0
        (SUM(IFF(Customers.is_customer_active_status == True, 1, 0)) * 100.0) / COUNT(Customers),
        0.0 # Default AR to 0.0 if no customers in the group (denominator is 0)
    )
)

# Step 5: Select only the country and the calculated AR for the final result.
final_result = ar_by_country.CALCULATE(
    country=output_country_name,
    AR=calculated_activity_ratio
)

[DEBUG] [Q12] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only "sell" types within the last month.
# The date range is from exactly one month ago up to the current moment.
sell_transactions_last_month = Broker.Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "sell") &
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-1 month')) &
    (date_time <= DATETIME('now'))
)

# Step 2: Augment these transactions with the ticker symbol for grouping.
# Fields 'amount', 'tax', 'commission' are carried over from sell_transactions_last_month.
transactions_with_symbol_for_partition = sell_transactions_last_month.CALCULATE(
    ticker_sym_val = ticker.symbol  # Extract ticker symbol for partitioning
)

# Step 3 & 4: Partition by ticker symbol and aggregate financial data.
# For each ticker group, calculate total sell amount, total tax, and total commission.
ticker_financial_summary = transactions_with_symbol_for_partition.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_group", by=(ticker_sym_val)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_output = ticker_sym_val,  # The ticker symbol from the partition key
    current_total_sell_amount = SUM(amount),    # Sum of amounts for this ticker
    current_total_tax = SUM(tax),              # Sum of taxes for this ticker
    current_total_commission = SUM(commission) # Sum of commissions for this ticker
)

# Step 5 & 6: Calculate SPM and select the final output fields.
# Handle division by zero by setting SPM to 0.0 if total sell amount is zero.
result = ticker_financial_summary.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker_symbol_output,
    SPM = IFF(
        current_total_sell_amount == 0,
        0.0,  # SPM is 0 if there's no sell amount to avoid division by zero
        (current_total_sell_amount - (current_total_tax + current_total_commission)) / current_total_sell_amount * 100.0
    )
)

[DEBUG] [Q12] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the period (1 month ago from now)
one_month_ago_timestamp = DATETIME('now', '-1 month')

# Start with Tickers, calculate relevant sell transactions for each
tickers_data = Tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_s=symbol,
    # Filter transactions_of for sell transactions in the last month
    sell_tx_last_month=transactions_of.WHERE(
        (transaction_type == "sell") & (date_time >= one_month_ago_timestamp)
    )
)

# Filter out tickers that have no such sell transactions
tickers_with_sells = tickers_data.WHERE(
    HAS(sell_tx_last_month) == 1
)

# Calculate aggregate values needed for SPM for the filtered tickers
aggregated_data = tickers_with_sells.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_out=ticker_s,
    total_amount_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.amount),
    total_tax_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.tax),
    total_commission_from_sells=SUM(sell_tx_last_month.commission)
)

# Calculate SPM using the aggregated values
spm_calculated_data = aggregated_data.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_final=ticker_symbol_out,
    # SPM formula: (Total Amount - (Tax + Commission)) / Total Amount * 100
    # Handle division by zero: if Total Amount is 0, SPM is 0.0
    calculated_spm=IFF(
        total_amount_from_sells == 0,
        0.0, # SPM is a percentage, so float
        (total_amount_from_sells - (total_tax_from_sells + total_commission_from_sells)) * 100.0 / total_amount_from_sells
    )
)

# Select the final required fields: ticker symbol and SPM
result = spm_calculated_data.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_final,
    spm=calculated_spm
)

    spm=((SUM(Transactions.amount) - SUM(Transactions.CALCULATE(cost=tax + commission).cost)) / SUM(Transactions.amount)) * 100
)

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only "sell" types within the last month.
# The date range is from exactly one month ago up to the current moment.
sell_transactions_last_month = Broker.Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "sell") &
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-1 month')) &
    (date_time <= DATETIME('now'))
)

# Step 2: Augment these transactions with the ticker symbol for grouping.
# Fields 'amount', 'tax', 'commission' are carried over from sell_transactions_last_month.
transactions_with_symbol_for_partition = sell_transactions_last_month.CALCULATE(
    ticker_sym_val = ticker.symbol  # Extract ticker symbol for partitioning
)

# Step 3 & 4: Partition by ticker symbol and aggregate financial data.
# For each ticker group, calculate total sell amount, total tax, and total commission.
ticker_financial_summary = transactions_with_symbol_for_partition.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_group", by=(ticker_sym_val)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_output = ticker_sym_val,  # The ticker symbol from the partition key
    current_total_sell_amount = SUM(amount),    # Sum of amounts for this ticker
    current_total_tax = SUM(tax),              # Sum of taxes for this ticker
    current_total_commission = SUM(commission) # Sum of commissions for this ticker
)

# Step 5 & 6: Calculate SPM and select the final output fields.
# Handle division by zero by setting SPM to 0.0 if total sell amount is zero.
result = ticker_financial_summary.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker_symbol_output,
    SPM = IFF(
        current_total_sell_amount == 0,
        0.0,  # SPM is 0 if there's no sell amount to avoid division by zero
        (current_total_sell_amount - (current_total_tax + current_total_commission)) / current_total_sell_amount * 100.0
    )
)

    symbol = symbol,
    month = month,
    avg_close = AVG(DailyPrices.close),

    calc_ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    calc_year=YEAR(date),
    calc_month=MONTH(date),
    calc_close_price=close,
    calc_high_price=high,
    calc_low_price=low
)

# Step 2: Partition by ticker symbol, year, and month to calculate monthly aggregates
monthly_aggregated_data = price_data_with_time_parts.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_price_group", by=(calc_ticker_symbol, calc_year, calc_month)
).CALCULATE(
    key_ticker_symbol=calc_ticker_symbol,
    key_year=calc_year,
    key_month=calc_month,
    agg_avg_close_price=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    agg_max_high_price=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    agg_min_low_price=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Partition the monthly aggregated data by ticker symbol to set up context for PREV function
ticker_partitioned_monthly_data = monthly_aggregated_data.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_group_for_momc", by=(key_ticker_symbol)
)

# Step 4: Access the sub-collection of monthly data per ticker and calculate previous month's average close price
# The sub-collection is named 'monthly_price_group' (the name of the partition in step 2).
data_with_prev_close = ticker_partitioned_monthly_data.monthly_price_group.CALCULATE(
    out_ticker_symbol=key_ticker_symbol, # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_year=key_year,                   # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_month=key_month,                 # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_avg_close_price=agg_avg_close_price, # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_max_high_price=agg_max_high_price,   # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    out_min_low_price=agg_min_low_price,     # Field from the sub-collection monthly_price_group
    calc_prev_month_avg_close=PREV(
        agg_avg_close_price,
        by=(key_year.ASC(), key_month.ASC()),
        per="ticker_group_for_momc", # Ensures PREV is calculated per ticker
        default=None
    )
)

# Step 5: Calculate MoMC using the previous month's average close and format the output
ticker_monthly_financial_summary = data_with_prev_close.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=out_ticker_symbol,
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", TEXT(out_year), LPAD(TEXT(out_month), 2, "0")),
    average_closing_price=out_avg_close_price,
    highest_price=out_max_high_price,
    lowest_price=out_min_low_price,
    MoMC=IFF(
        calc_prev_month_avg_close == None,
        None,
        (out_avg_close_price - calc_prev_month_avg_close) / calc_prev_month_avg_close
    )
).ORDER_BY(ticker_symbol.ASC(), month.ASC())

# Step 1: Prepare daily price details with year, month, and ticker symbol
price_details = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_val=ticker.symbol,
    year_val=YEAR(date),
    month_val=MONTH(date),
    year_month_str_val=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0"))
    # DailyPrices.close, DailyPrices.high, DailyPrices.low are implicitly available for aggregation
)

# Step 2: Outer partition by ticker symbol. This creates the 'ticker_level_data' context.
ticker_partition = price_details.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_level_data", # Name for the ticker-level grouping, used by PREV's 'per'
    by=(ticker_symbol_val)
)

# Step 3: Inner partition by month on the sub-collection of daily prices for each ticker.
# Then calculate monthly aggregates for each ticker.
# ticker_partition.price_details refers to the 'price_details' records for the current ticker group.
monthly_data_for_ticker = ticker_partition.price_details.PARTITION(
    name="month_level_data", # Name for the month-level grouping within each ticker
    by=(year_val, month_val, year_month_str_val) # Partition keys from price_details
).CALCULATE(
    # Carry over ticker symbol from the outer partition's scope
    ticker_symbol_calc=ticker_symbol_val,
    # Carry over year, month, and year_month_str from the inner partition's keys
    year_calc=year_val,
    month_calc=month_val,
    year_month_str_calc=year_month_str_val,
    # Calculate monthly aggregates using original DailyPrices fields within the group
    avg_close_price_calc=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high_price_calc=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low_price_calc=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 4: Calculate PREV (previous month's average close) on the monthly aggregated data.
# This operates on 'monthly_data_for_ticker', which is effectively per-ticker due to the structure.
# PREV uses 'per="ticker_level_data"' to ensure it's calculated within each ticker group.
data_with_prev_close = monthly_data_for_ticker.CALCULATE(
    # Carry over all necessary fields
    ticker_symbol_carry=ticker_symbol_calc,
    year_carry=year_calc,
    month_carry=month_calc,
    year_month_str_carry=year_month_str_calc,
    avg_close_price_carry=avg_close_price_calc,
    max_high_price_carry=max_high_price_calc,
    min_low_price_carry=min_low_price_calc,

    # Calculate previous month's average closing price
    prev_avg_close_val=PREV(
        avg_close_price_calc, # Expression to get previous value of
        by=(year_calc.ASC(), month_calc.ASC()), # Order within each ticker group for PREV
        per="ticker_level_data", # Specifies that PREV operates per group of the 'ticker_level_data' partition
        default=None # Value if no previous record (e.g., first month for a ticker)
    )
)

# Step 5: Calculate MoMC and select final output fields
final_result_unsorted = data_with_prev_close.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_carry,
    month=year_month_str_carry, # Use the "YYYY-MM" string format for month
    average_closing_price=avg_close_price_carry,
    highest_price=max_high_price_carry,
    lowest_price=min_low_price_carry,
    # Calculate MoMC, handling cases where prev_avg_close_val is None or zero
    momc=IFF(
        (prev_avg_close_val == None) | (prev_avg_close_val == 0),
        None, # MoMC is not applicable or undefined
        (avg_close_price_carry - prev_avg_close_val) / prev_avg_close_val
    ),
    # Keep original year and month for sorting if they are not part of the final SELECT
    year_for_sort=year_carry,
    month_for_sort=month_carry
)

# Step 6: Sort the final result
ticker_monthly_momc_analysis = final_result_unsorted.ORDER_BY(
    ticker_symbol.ASC(),
    year_for_sort.ASC(),
    month_for_sort.ASC()
).CALCULATE( # Final projection to include only the requested columns
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    month=month,
    average_closing_price=average_closing_price,
    highest_price=highest_price,
    lowest_price=lowest_price,
    momc=momc
)

# Step 1: Prepare data from DailyPrices, extracting year, month, symbol, and relevant price fields.
price_info_for_agg = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    calc_year = YEAR(date),
    calc_month = MONTH(date),
    calc_symbol = ticker.symbol  # Get the ticker symbol directly
    # Original price fields (close, high, low) will be accessed via DailyPrices collection in the aggregation step
)

# Step 2: Partition by symbol, year, and month to calculate monthly aggregates.
# The result, 'monthly_stats', will have one row per unique symbol-year-month combination.
monthly_stats = price_info_for_agg.PARTITION(name="monthly_group", by=(calc_symbol, calc_year, calc_month)).CALCULATE(
    stat_symbol = calc_symbol,    # Field from the 'by' clause of the partition
    stat_year = calc_year,      # Field from the 'by' clause of the partition
    stat_month = calc_month,    # Field from the 'by' clause of the partition
    avg_close = AVG(DailyPrices.close),  # Aggregate over the 'DailyPrices' data within the current partition group
    max_high = MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low = MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Partition the 'monthly_stats' by ticker symbol to prepare for MoMC calculation.
# This creates a context where 'PREV' can operate per ticker.
# 'ticker_series' is the name of this partition group.
ticker_series_partition = monthly_stats.PARTITION(name="ticker_series", by=(stat_symbol))

# Step 4: Calculate MoMC using the PREV window function.
# This operates on the 'monthly_stats' sub-collection within each 'ticker_series' partition group.
data_with_momc = ticker_series_partition.monthly_stats.CALCULATE(
    # Carry forward fields from 'monthly_stats' for the final output
    final_symbol = stat_symbol,
    final_year = stat_year,
    final_month = stat_month,
    final_avg_close = avg_close,
    final_max_high = max_high,
    final_min_low = min_low,

    # Get the average closing price of the previous month for the same ticker
    # PREV is ordered by year and month, and scoped per "ticker_series" (i.e., per ticker symbol)
    prev_avg_c = PREV(avg_close, by=(stat_year.ASC(), stat_month.ASC()), per="ticker_series", default=None),

    # Calculate MoMC: (current_avg_close - prev_avg_close) / prev_avg_close
    # If prev_avg_c is None (no previous month), MoMC is None.
    momc_val = IFF(
        prev_avg_c != None,
        (avg_close - prev_avg_c) / prev_avg_c,
        None
    )
)

# Step 5: Select and format the final output fields, and order the results.
# The month is formatted as 'YYYY-MM'.
result = data_with_momc.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = final_symbol,
    month = JOIN_STRINGS("-", final_year, LPAD(final_month, 2, "0")), # LPAD ensures month is two digits (e.g., '01' for January)
    average_closing_price = final_avg_close,
    highest_price = final_max_high,
    lowest_price = final_min_low,
    MoMC = momc_val
).ORDER_BY(final_symbol.ASC(), final_year.ASC(), final_month.ASC()) # Order chronologically per ticker

stocks_data = Tickers.CALCULATE(
    ticker_s_val=symbol,
    # Filter historical_prices for dates between 2023-04-01 and 2023-04-04 inclusive.
    prices_in_range=historical_prices.WHERE(
        (date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")
    )
)

# Filter out stocks that have no historical prices in the specified date range.
stocks_with_prices_in_range = stocks_data.WHERE(
    HAS(prices_in_range) == 1
)

# For the remaining stocks, calculate the maximum high and minimum low from their prices_in_range.
# These aggregations (MAX, MIN) are performed on the 'prices_in_range' sub-collection for each stock.
stock_extremes_in_range = stocks_with_prices_in_range.CALCULATE(
    ticker_s_val=ticker_s_val,
    max_high_val=MAX(prices_in_range.high),
    min_low_val=MIN(prices_in_range.low)
)

# Calculate the price change (difference between max_high_val and min_low_val).
stock_price_diffs = stock_extremes_in_range.CALCULATE(
    ticker_s_val=ticker_s_val,
    price_diff_val=max_high_val - min_low_val
)

# Select the top 3 stocks with the largest price_diff_val.
# The .DESC() ensures that higher price changes are ranked first.
top_3_stocks_by_price_change = stock_price_diffs.TOP_K(3, by=price_diff_val.DESC())

# Prepare the final result with the requested fields: ticker_symbol and price_change.
result = top_3_stocks_by_price_change.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_s_val,
    price_change=price_diff_val
)

# Calculate join month/year for each customer and count transactions in that same month
customer_join_month_transactions = Customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_id=_id,
    customer_name=name,
    join_year=YEAR(join_date),
    join_month=MONTH(join_date),
    transactions_in_join_month=COUNT(
        transactions_made.WHERE(

            (MONTH(date_time) == MONTH(join_date))
        )

).WHERE(transactions_in_join_month > 0)

# Filter transactions to the specified date range (Jan 1, 2023 to Mar 31, 2023)
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (date_time >= "2023-01-01") & (date_time <= "2023-03-31")
)

# Group by transaction type and calculate metrics
transaction_type_stats = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="transaction_types", 
    by=transaction_type
).CALCULATE(

    distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),

# Step 1: Filter transactions to the specified date range.
# We use explicit timestamp boundaries to ensure inclusivity of the start and end dates.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (date_time >= "2023-01-01T00:00:00.000") & (date_time <= "2023-03-31T23:59:59.999")
)

# Step 2: Group by transaction_type and calculate the required metrics.
# - 'transaction_type' is the partitioning key.
# - 'num_distinct_customers' counts unique customer_id values within each group from the filtered Transactions.
# - 'avg_num_shares' calculates the average of shares within each group from the filtered Transactions.
transaction_summary = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(name="txn_groups", by=(transaction_type)).CALCULATE(
    type_of_transaction = transaction_type,
    num_distinct_customers = NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),
    avg_num_shares = AVG(Transactions.shares)
)

# Step 3: Select the top 3 transaction types based on the number of distinct customers.
# The results are ordered in descending order of num_distinct_customers.
top_3_transaction_types_by_customer_count = transaction_summary.TOP_K(3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC())

# The variable 'top_3_transaction_types_by_customer_count' now holds the desired result.
result = top_3_transaction_types_by_customer_count

# Step 1: Filter transactions to the specified date range.
# We compare the date part of 'date_time' by truncating it to the 'start of day'.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATETIME(date_time, 'start of day') >= DATETIME('2023-01-01', 'start of day')) &
    (DATETIME(date_time, 'start of day') <= DATETIME('2023-03-31', 'start of day'))
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by 'transaction_type'.
# For each type, calculate the number of distinct customers and the average number of shares.
transaction_type_summary = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="txn_type_groups", by=(transaction_type)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,  # The partition key
    num_distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id), # Count distinct customers in this group
    avg_num_shares=AVG(Transactions.shares)  # Calculate average shares for this group
)

# Step 3: Select the top 3 transaction types by the number of distinct customers.
# Then, select the required fields for the final output.
result = transaction_type_summary.TOP_K(
    3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC()
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,
    num_distinct_customers=num_distinct_customers,
    avg_shares=avg_num_shares # Renaming for the final output as "average number of shares"
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by transaction_type.
# For each type, calculate the number of distinct customers and the average number of shares.
transaction_type_summary = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="type_groups", by=(transaction_type)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,  # The transaction type from the partition key
    num_distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),  # Count distinct customers for this transaction type
    avg_shares=AVG(Transactions.shares)  # Calculate average shares for this transaction type
)

# Step 3: Select the top 3 transaction types based on the number of distinct customers.
# The result will include transaction_type, num_distinct_customers, and avg_shares.
result = transaction_type_summary.TOP_K(
    3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC()
)

[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only those from the last 30 days (inclusive).
recent_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(DATEDIFF("days", date_time, 'now') <= 30)

# Step 2: For these transactions, calculate the customer's country.
# This 'customer_country_field' will be used as the partition key.
transactions_with_country_info = recent_transactions.CALCULATE(
    customer_country_field = customer.country
)

# Step 3 & 4: Partition by customer country and calculate aggregates for each country.
# - country_name: The name of the country.
# - num_transactions: The count of transactions for that country.
# - total_amount: The sum of transaction amounts for that country.
country_summary_data = transactions_with_country_info.PARTITION(
    name="country_group", by=(customer_country_field)
).CALCULATE(
    country_name = customer_country_field,
    number_of_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(Transactions.amount)
)

# Step 5: Select the top 5 countries based on the total transaction amount, in descending order.
top_countries_by_transaction_amount = country_summary_data.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
)

[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Annotate each transaction with the customer's country.
# 'customer.country' accesses the country of the customer associated with the transaction.
# 'date_time' and 'amount' are original fields of the Transactions collection and are implicitly available.
transactions_with_country_details = Transactions.CALCULATE(
    customer_country_field = customer.country
)

# Step 2: Filter transactions to include only those from the last 30 days.
# DATEDIFF("days", date_time, 'now') calculates the number of full days between the transaction's date_time and now.
# '<= 30' ensures we include transactions from today up to 30 days ago.
recent_transactions_data = transactions_with_country_details.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", date_time, 'now') <= 30
)

# Step 3: Group the filtered transactions by country and calculate aggregates.
# PARTITION groups transactions by 'customer_country_field'.
# For each group (country):
#   - 'country_name' is the name of the country from the partition key.
#   - 'num_transactions' is the count of transactions in that country's group.
#   - 'total_transaction_amount' is the sum of the 'amount' for all transactions in that country's group.
country_aggregated_summary = recent_transactions_data.PARTITION(
    name="country_groups", by=(customer_country_field)
).CALCULATE(
    country_name = customer_country_field,
    num_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(Transactions.amount)
)

# Step 4: Select the top 5 countries based on the total transaction amount.
# TOP_K sorts the results by 'total_transaction_amount' in descending order and takes the first 5.
# The output columns (country_name, num_transactions, total_transaction_amount) are preserved from the previous step.
top_countries_by_transaction_volume = country_aggregated_summary.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
)

result = top_countries_by_transaction_volume

[DEBUG] [Q29] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter customers with transactions and prepare necessary timestamp fields.
# - customer_join_timestamp: Converts the join_date (date) to a timestamp (start of day).
# - first_tx_timestamp: Gets the timestamp of the customer's very first transaction.
customers_with_tx_timestamps = Customers.WHERE(HAS(transactions_made) == 1).CALCULATE(
    customer_join_timestamp=DATETIME(join_date, 'start of day'),
    first_tx_timestamp=transactions_made.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=date_time.ASC(), per="Customers", allow_ties=True) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().date_time
)

# Step 2: Calculate the difference in seconds between joining and the first transaction.
# The _id from the Customers collection is carried forward.
customers_with_diff_seconds = customers_with_tx_timestamps.CALCULATE(
    _id=_id, # Carry over customer _id
    customer_join_timestamp=customer_join_timestamp, # Carry over for next step if needed, or just use directly
    first_tx_timestamp=first_tx_timestamp, # Carry over for next step
    diff_in_seconds_val=DATEDIFF("seconds", customer_join_timestamp, first_tx_timestamp)
)

# Step 3: Convert the difference from seconds to days (with decimals) and select final output fields.
# We use diff_in_seconds_val calculated in the previous step.
result = customers_with_diff_seconds.CALCULATE(
    _id=_id, # Select the customer's _id
    time_difference_days=diff_in_seconds_val / 86400.0 # Convert seconds to days (24.0 * 60.0 * 60.0)
)

result = Customers.WHERE(HAS(transactions_made) == 1).CALCULATE(
    # Select the customer's _id
    customer_id=_id,
    # Calculate the time difference between join_date and the first transaction date_time in decimal days.
    # First, find the minimum (earliest) transaction date_time for the customer.
    # Then, calculate the difference in seconds between the join_date and this first transaction date_time.
    # Finally, convert seconds to days by dividing by 86400.0 (24 hours * 60 minutes * 60 seconds).
    time_difference_decimal_days=(
        DATEDIFF(
            "seconds",
            join_date, # Customer's join date
            MIN(transactions_made.date_time) # Date_time of the customer's first transaction
        ) / 86400.0
    )
)

# Filter transactions for successful ones in Q1 2023
q1_2023_successful_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (status == 'success') &
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
    (MONTH(date_time) >= 1) &
    (MONTH(date_time) <= 3)
).CALCULATE(
    month = MONTH(date_time),
    transaction_price = price
)

# Group by month and calculate average price
result = q1_2023_successful_transactions.PARTITION(name="months", by=month).CALCULATE(
    month = month,
    avg_transaction_price = AVG(Transactions.price)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q31] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions for status 'success' and Q1 2023, and extract the transaction month.
# Q1 2023 includes months 1 (January), 2 (February), and 3 (March).
filtered_transactions_with_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (status == 'success') &
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
    (MONTH(date_time) >= 1) & (MONTH(date_time) <= 3)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month = MONTH(date_time)
    # The 'price' field is an original field of Transactions and will be available for aggregation.
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by month and calculate the average transaction price for each month.
# The result is ordered by month in ascending order.
monthly_avg_transaction_price = filtered_transactions_with_month.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_grouped_transactions", by=(transaction_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month = transaction_month,  # Output the month
    average_price = AVG(Transactions.price)  # Calculate the average price for transactions in this month group
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

result = monthly_avg_transaction_price

# Step 1: Filter transactions for successful ones in Q1 2023 and extract the month.
# We filter by status 'success', year 2023, and months 1, 2, or 3.
# We also calculate 'transaction_month' which will be used as a key for partitioning.
q1_2023_successful_transactions_with_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (status == 'success') &
    (YEAR(date_time) == 2023) &
    ISIN(MONTH(date_time), (1, 2, 3))
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month = MONTH(date_time)
    # The 'price' field is an original field of Transactions and will be used for aggregation.
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by month and calculate the average transaction price.
# We group by 'transaction_month' (the partition key).
# For each month, we calculate AVG(Transactions.price) which computes the average of the 'price'
# for all transactions within that month's group.
result = q1_2023_successful_transactions_with_month.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_data", by=(transaction_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month = transaction_month,  # Output the month (which was the partition key)
    average_transaction_price = AVG(Transactions.price)  # Calculate the average price for transactions in this month
)

Return the week (as a date), total payments received, and weekend payments received in ascending order.

[DEBUG] [Q32] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
INFO:google_genai.models:AFC is enabled with max remote calls: 10.
INFO:httpx:HTTP Request: POST https://us-east5-aiplatform.googleapis.com/v1/projects/solid-drive-448717-p8/locations/us-east5/publishers/anthropic/models/claude-sonnet-4@20250514:streamRawPredict "HTTP/1.1 200 OK"
[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by claude:
# Get the date range for last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
date_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-0 weeks'),
    eight_weeks_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-9 weeks')
)

# Get payments from high-value sales in the target date range
high_value_payments = date_info.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= eight_weeks_ago) & 
    (payment_date < current_week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (1, 7))
)

# Group by week and calculate totals
result = high_value_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

result = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    # Define the boundaries for the last 8 calendar weeks, excluding the current week.
    # report_period_start_date_val is the start date of the week that began 8 weeks before the current week's start.
    # For example, if the current week started on 2023-01-15, this would be 2022-11-21 (Monday, 8 weeks prior).
    report_period_start_date_val = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-8 weeks'),
    # report_period_end_date_exclusive_val is the start date of the current week.
    # Payments must occur *before* this date to be included in the previous weeks.
    report_period_end_date_exclusive_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
).Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    # Filter payments to include only those within the defined 8-week period.
    (payment_date >= report_period_start_date_val) & (payment_date < report_period_end_date_exclusive_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # For each filtered payment, determine the start date of the week it belongs to.
    payment_week_start_dt_calc = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Determine if the payment was made on a weekend (Saturday or Sunday).
    # This will be 1 if it's a weekend payment, 0 otherwise.
    is_weekend_payment_num_calc = IFF(ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday")), 1, 0)
).PARTITION(
    # Group the payments by the calculated week start date.
    name="weekly_payments_group",
    by=(payment_week_start_dt_calc)
).CALCULATE(
    # For each week (each partition group):
    # week_out: The start date of the week (this is the partition key).
    week_out = payment_week_start_dt_calc,
    # total_payments_received_out: Count all payments within this week's group.
    total_payments_received_out = COUNT(),
    # weekend_payments_received_out: Sum the 'is_weekend_payment_num_calc' flags.
    # This effectively counts payments made on a weekend within this week's group.
    weekend_payments_received_out = SUM(is_weekend_payment_num_calc)
).ORDER_BY(week_out.ASC()).CALCULATE(
    # Select and rename the fields for the final output, ensuring results are ordered by week.
    week = week_out,
    total_payments_received = total_payments_received_out,
    weekend_payments_received = weekend_payments_received_out
)

# Get the date range for last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
date_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-0 weeks'),
    eight_weeks_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-9 weeks')
)

# Get payments from high-value sales in the target date range
high_value_payments = date_info.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= eight_weeks_ago) & 
    (payment_date < current_week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (1, 7))
)

# Group by week and calculate totals
result = high_value_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Define date boundaries for the analysis period.
# 'analysis_start_dt' is the start of the week, 8 weeks prior to the current week's start.
# 'analysis_end_dt' is the end of the last full week (i.e., one day before the current week's start).
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    # Using DATETIME('now', 'start of week') to get the start of the current week.
    # Then, subtract 8 weeks to find the start of the 8-week-ago period.
    analysis_start_dt = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-8 weeks'),
    # End of the analysis period is one day before the start of the current week.
    analysis_end_dt = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-1 day')
)

# 1. Filter sales with sale_price > $30,000.
# 2. Access their associated payments. The date_boundaries are available from the context.
payments_from_high_value_sales = date_boundaries.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000.0).payment

# 3. Filter these payments to include only those within the last 8 full calendar weeks.
# 4. For each relevant payment, determine its week's start date and if it occurred on a weekend.
payments_in_analysis_period = payments_from_high_value_sales.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= analysis_start_dt) & (payment_date <= analysis_end_dt)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_for_payment = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_payment_on_weekend = ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# 5. Group the filtered payments by their week's start date.
#    For each week, calculate the total number of payments and the number of weekend payments.
weekly_payment_stats = payments_in_analysis_period.PARTITION(
    name="payments_grouped_by_week", by=(week_start_for_payment)
).CALCULATE(
    week_iso_date = week_start_for_payment,  # The partition key, representing the start of the week.
    total_payments_in_week = COUNT(payments_in_analysis_period), # Total payments in this week's group.
    weekend_payments_in_week = SUM(IFF(payments_in_analysis_period.is_payment_on_weekend, 1, 0)) # Sum of weekend payments (1 if true, 0 if false).
).ORDER_BY(week_iso_date.ASC()) # 6. Order results by week in ascending order.

result = weekly_payment_stats

# Filter cars from the Dealership collection where the VIN contains 'M5' (case-insensitive).
# The LIKE function with UPPER ensures case-insensitivity and partial matching.
cars_filtered_by_vin = Dealership.Cars.WHERE(LIKE(UPPER(vin_number), '%M5%'))

# Partition the filtered cars by their make and model.
# For each unique make/model group, calculate the make, model, and the total number of sales.
result = cars_filtered_by_vin.PARTITION(
    name="model_group",  # Assigns a name to the collection of groups created by the partition
    by=(make, model)     # Specifies the fields to partition by (car make and model)
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,       # Selects the 'make' from the partition key
    car_model=model,     # Selects the 'model' from the partition key
    num_sales=COUNT(Cars.sale_records)  # Counts the number of sales records for cars within the current make/model group
)

# Filter sales for Toyota cars made in the last 30 days (inclusive of today)
toyota_sales_in_last_30_days = Sales.WHERE(
    (Sales.car.make == "Toyota") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", Sales.sale_date, DATETIME('now')) >= 0) &  # Ensures sale_date is not in the future
    (DATEDIFF("days", Sales.sale_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 29)   # Sale occurred between 0 and 29 days ago
)

# Calculate the number of sales and total revenue
result = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    number_of_toyota_sales = COUNT(toyota_sales_in_last_30_days),
    total_revenue_from_toyota_sales = SUM(toyota_sales_in_last_30_days.sale_price)
)

# Step 1: Calculate aggregated sales data for each salesperson.
# This includes their first name, last name, the sum of their sales (total_sales_amount),
# and the count of sales they made (number_of_sales).
sales_data_per_salesperson = Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    sp_fn=first_name,  # Temporary alias for first_name
    sp_ln=last_name,   # Temporary alias for last_name
    agg_total_sales=SUM(sales_made.sale_price),  # Sum of sale_price from all sales_made by the salesperson
    agg_num_sales=COUNT(sales_made)              # Count of sales_made by the salesperson
)

# Step 2: Calculate the sales rank (SR) and format the final output.
# The SR is determined by ranking salespersons based on their agg_total_sales in descending order.
# The result is then ordered by SR in ascending order to show the top salesperson first.
final_result = sales_data_per_salesperson.CALCULATE(
    first_name=sp_fn,                        # Final field: first_name
    last_name=sp_ln,                         # Final field: last_name
    total_sales_amount=agg_total_sales,      # Final field: total_sales_amount
    number_of_sales=agg_num_sales,           # Final field: number_of_sales
    SR=RANKING(by=agg_total_sales.DESC())    # Calculate Sales Rank (SR)
).ORDER_BY(SR.ASC())                         # Order the results by SR (ascending)

# First, get cars with their most recent inventory status
cars_with_latest_inventory = Cars.WHERE(
    HAS(sale_records) == 1 & HAS(inventory_snapshots) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,
    car_model=model,
    latest_inventory_status=inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC()) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory,
    max_sale_price=MAX(sale_records.sale_price)
)

# Filter for cars no longer in inventory and group by make/model
result = cars_with_latest_inventory.WHERE(
    latest_inventory_status == False
).PARTITION(name="make_model_groups", by=(car_make, car_model)).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.max_sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q37] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: For each sale, gather car's make, model, and its latest inventory status.
# Sales.car refers to the car associated with a given sale.
# Sales.car.inventory_snapshots provides the inventory history for that specific car.
# RANKING() without 'per' operates on the current collection (snapshots of the specific car).
sales_info = Sales.CALCULATE(
    sale_p=sale_price,  # Store the sale price
    car_mk=Sales.car.make,  # Get the make of the car involved in the sale
    car_mdl=Sales.car.model,  # Get the model of the car involved in the sale
    # Determine the latest inventory status for the car from its snapshots
    car_latest_status=Sales.car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1  # Find the latest snapshot(s)
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory  # Get the inventory status from one such latest snapshot
)

# Step 2: Filter sales to include only those where the car is no longer in inventory.
# A car is "sold" because it's in the Sales collection.
# "No longer in inventory" means car_latest_status is False.
# If car_latest_status is null (no snapshots) or True, the sale is excluded.
filtered_sales_records = sales_info.WHERE(
    car_latest_status == False
)

# Step 3: Group the filtered sales by car make and model.
# For each unique make/model combination, calculate the highest sale price.
make_model_max_prices = filtered_sales_records.PARTITION(
    name="sales_by_make_model", by=(car_mk, car_mdl)  # Group by make and model
).CALCULATE(
    make_name=car_mk,  # Output the make
    model_name=car_mdl,  # Output the model
    highest_sale_p=MAX(sale_p)  # Calculate the maximum sale price for this group
)

# Step 4: Order the results by the highest sale price, from highest to lowest.
final_result = make_model_max_prices.ORDER_BY(highest_sale_p.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q37] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Start from the Sales collection. For each sale, retrieve information about the car,
# its make, model, and determine its latest inventory status.
sales_with_car_details = Sales.CALCULATE(
    # Store the sale price for later aggregation.
    sale_price_val=sale_price,
    # Get the make and model of the car involved in the sale.
    car_make_val=car.make,
    car_model_val=car.model,
    # Determine if the car is no longer in inventory based on its latest snapshot.
    # Access the car's inventory_snapshots.
    # RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1 finds the latest snapshot(s) for this specific car.
    # .SINGULAR() selects one if multiple (e.g., same date, though is_in_inventory should be consistent).
    # Check if its is_in_inventory status is False.
    is_car_no_longer_in_inventory=(car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory == False)
)

# Step 2: Filter these sales records to include only those where the car
# is confirmed to be no longer in inventory.
# Cars in the Sales collection are inherently "sold".
filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory = sales_with_car_details.WHERE(
    sales_with_car_details.is_car_no_longer_in_inventory == True
)

# Step 3: Group these filtered sales by car make and model.
# For each group, calculate the maximum sale price.
# The collection being partitioned is `filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory`.
# `MAX(filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.sale_price_val)` will compute the max sale_price_val
# from the sales records within each (make, model) group.
max_sale_prices_by_make_model = filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_group", by=(car_make_val, car_model_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # Output make and model from the partition keys.
    output_make=car_make_val,
    output_model=car_model_val,
    # Calculate the highest sale price for this group.
    output_highest_sale_price=MAX(filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.sale_price_val)
)

# Step 4: Select the final required fields (make, model, highest_sale_price)
# and order the results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = max_sale_prices_by_make_model.CALCULATE(
    make=output_make,
    model=output_model,
    highest_sale_price=output_highest_sale_price
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

Truncate date to month for aggregation.

# Filter salespersons hired between 2022-2023 (inclusive)
eligible_salespersons = Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Get sales from these salespersons in the last 6 months excluding current month
# Using DATETIME to get the date range
sales_in_period = eligible_salespersons.sales_made.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME("now")) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME("now")) <= 6)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, "start of month"),
    sale_price = sale_price,
    salesperson_id = salesperson_id
)

# Group by month and calculate metrics
monthly_metrics = sales_in_period.PARTITION(name="months", by=sale_month).CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month,
    PMSPS = COUNT(Sales),
    PMSR = SUM(Sales.sale_price)
)

# To ensure all 6 months are represented, we need to create a complete month range
# and left join with our results
result = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Define Date Boundaries for the reporting period (last 6 full months, excluding current month)
current_month_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
# period_start_dt will be the first day of the month, 6 months ago.
# e.g., if current_month_start_dt is July 1st, period_start_dt will be January 1st.
period_start_dt = DATETIME(current_month_start_dt, '-6 months')
# period_end_exclusive_dt will be the first day of the current month.
# Sales up to, but not including, this date will be considered.
period_end_exclusive_dt = current_month_start_dt

# Step 1: Create a scaffold of all unique months within the desired reporting period.
# This is based on all sales in the system to ensure all calendar months with any sale are represented.
all_sales_with_month_info = Dealership.Sales.CALCULATE(
    sale_month_val = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') # Truncate sale_date to the start of its month
)

# Get distinct months from all sales and filter them to the 6-month reporting window.
# These distinct months will form the rows of our report.
distinct_months_for_report = all_sales_with_month_info.PARTITION(
    name="month_partition", by=(sale_month_val) # Group by the calculated sale_month_val to get distinct months
).CALCULATE(
    report_month_value = sale_month_val # Select the distinct month value
).WHERE(
    (report_month_value >= period_start_dt) & (report_month_value < period_end_exclusive_dt) # Filter to the defined 6-month period
)

# Step 2: For each month in our scaffold, calculate PMSPS and PMSR.
# Metrics are specific to salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023.
monthly_metrics = distinct_months_for_report.CALCULATE(
    # The month being reported on
    Month = report_month_value,

    # PMSPS: Per Month Salesperson Sales count
    # Count sales made by salespersons hired in 2022-2023, where the sale_date (truncated to month) matches report_month_value.
    PMSPS = COUNT(
        Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
            (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023) # Filter salespersons by hire year
        ).sales_made.WHERE(
            DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == report_month_value # Filter their sales to the specific month from the scaffold
        )
    ),

    # PMSR: Per Month Sales Revenue
    # Sum of sale_price for the same set of filtered sales.
    PMSR = DEFAULT_TO( # Use DEFAULT_TO to ensure 0.0 if there are no sales, as SUM might return null.
        SUM(
            Dealership.Salespersons.WHERE(
                (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023) # Filter salespersons by hire year
            ).sales_made.WHERE(
                DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == report_month_value # Filter their sales to the specific month
            ).sale_price # Access the sale_price for summation
        ),
        0.0 # Default to 0.0 if the sum is null (i.e., no sales in that month for these salespersons)
    )
)

# Step 3: Order the results by month in ascending order.
final_report = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(Month.ASC())

# Filter salespersons hired between 2022-2023 (inclusive)
eligible_salespersons = Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Get sales from these salespersons in the last 6 months excluding current month
# Calculate the date range: from 6 months ago to start of current month
sales_in_period = eligible_salespersons.sales_made.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 6)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    salesperson_id=salesperson_id,
    sale_price=sale_price
)

# Group by month and calculate metrics
monthly_metrics = sales_in_period.PARTITION(name="months", by=sale_month).CALCULATE(
    month=sale_month,
    pmsps=COUNT(Sales.salesperson_id),  # Per month salesperson sales count
    pmsr=SUM(Sales.sale_price)          # Per month sales revenue
)

# To ensure all 6 months are included (even with 0 values), we need to create
# a complete month range and left join with our metrics
result = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q39] Code generated by gemini:
# Define date boundaries for the reporting period
v_six_months_ago_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
v_current_month_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Prepare sales data: extract month, salesperson hire year, and sale price
base_sales_data = Sales.CALCULATE(
    calc_sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    calc_sales_person_hire_year = YEAR(salesperson.hire_date),
    calc_sale_price = sale_price
)

# Filter sales to be within the last 6 months (excluding current month)
filtered_sales_data = base_sales_data.WHERE(
    (calc_sale_month >= v_six_months_ago_start_dt) & (calc_sale_month < v_current_month_start_dt)
)

# Partition the filtered sales by month.
# This creates groups for each month that has at least one sale in the filtered_sales_data.
partitioned_by_month = filtered_sales_data.PARTITION(name="mg", by=(calc_sale_month))

# For each month group, calculate PMSPS and PMSR for salespersons hired in 2022-2023
result = partitioned_by_month.CALCULATE(
    res_report_month = calc_sale_month,  # The month being reported

    # Within each month's partition group (mg.filtered_sales_data),
    # filter for sales made by salespersons hired in the target years.
    calc_valid_sales_for_month = mg.filtered_sales_data.WHERE(
        (calc_sales_person_hire_year >= 2022) & (calc_sales_person_hire_year <= 2023)
    ),

    PMSPS = COUNT(calc_valid_sales_for_month),
    PMSR = DEFAULT_TO(SUM(calc_valid_sales_for_month.calc_sale_price), 0.0)
).ORDER_BY(res_report_month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q44] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Add year and month columns to PaymentsReceived data
payments_with_year_month = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    pay_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    pay_month=MONTH(payment_date)
    # payment_amount is an original field and will be available
)

# Step 2: Group by year and month, then calculate the sum of payments for each group.
# Order the results chronologically for the PREV function.
monthly_aggregated_payments = payments_with_year_month.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_payment_group", by=(pay_year, pay_month)
).CALCULATE(
    pay_year=pay_year,  # This is the partition key
    pay_month=pay_month, # This is the partition key
    total_monthly_payment_amount=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount) # Sum amounts from original table for the current group
).ORDER_BY(pay_year.ASC(), pay_month.ASC())

# Step 3: Calculate the Month-over-Month (MoM) change.
# The PREV function looks at the previous record in the ordered dataset.
result = monthly_aggregated_payments.CALCULATE(
    payment_year=pay_year,
    payment_month=pay_month,
    total_payments_for_month=total_monthly_payment_amount,
    # MoM change = current month's total - previous month's total.
    # PREV(total_monthly_payment_amount, by=(...)) retrieves the total_monthly_payment_amount
    # from the previous row based on the specified ordering.
    # If there's no previous row (i.e., for the first month), PREV returns None,
    # and (value - None) results in None, which is appropriate.
    month_over_month_change=total_monthly_payment_amount - PREV(total_monthly_payment_amount, by=(pay_year.ASC(), pay_month.ASC()))
)

# Step 1: Extract year and month from payment_date for each payment, and keep the payment amount.
payments_with_period = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    pay_year_val=YEAR(payment_date),
    pay_month_val=MONTH(payment_date),
    amount=payment_amount
)

# Step 2: Group by year and month. For each group, sum the payment amounts.
# This creates a collection where each row represents a unique year-month and its total payments.
monthly_aggregated_payments = payments_with_period.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups", by=(pay_year_val, pay_month_val)
).CALCULATE(
    year_num=pay_year_val,
    month_num=pay_month_val,
    # Sums the 'amount' from the 'payments_with_period' collection for records within the current partition group.
    current_month_total_payments=SUM(payments_with_period.amount)
)

# Step 3: Calculate previous month's payments using the PREV window function.
# This operates on the 'monthly_aggregated_payments' collection.
result_with_mom_calc = monthly_aggregated_payments.CALCULATE(
    calc_year=year_num,
    calc_month=month_num,
    calc_total_payments=current_month_total_payments,
    # PREV fetches 'current_month_total_payments' from the previous record,
    # ordered by year and then month. 'default=0.0' is used for the first month in the dataset.
    calc_prev_month_payments=PREV(current_month_total_payments, by=(year_num.ASC(), month_num.ASC()), default=0.0)
)

# Step 4: Calculate the final MoM change and select the desired output fields.
# Order the results chronologically.
monthly_analysis = result_with_mom_calc.CALCULATE(
    year=calc_year,
    month=calc_month,
    total_payments_received=calc_total_payments,
    MoM_change=calc_total_payments - calc_prev_month_payments  # MoM change = current month - previous month
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())
