# Step 3: Calculate the Month-over-Month (MoM) change.
# First, retrieve the previous month's total payment amount using the PREV window function.
# The PREV function requires the data to be ordered, which is done by payment_year and payment_month.
# Then, calculate the MoM change.
# The final result is ordered by year and month.
final_result = monthly_aggregated_payments.CALCULATE(
    year_of_payment=payment_year,
    month_of_payment=payment_month,
    total_payments_for_month=total_monthly_payment_amount,
    # PREV fetches total_monthly_payment_amount from the previous row based on year/month ordering.
    # If it's the first month in the dataset, prev_month_total_amount will be None.
    prev_month_total_amount=PREV(total_monthly_payment_amount, by=(payment_year.ASC(), payment_month.ASC()))
).CALCULATE(
    year_of_payment=year_of_payment,
    month_of_payment=month_of_payment,
    total_payments_for_month=total_payments_for_month,
    # MoM change = current month's total - previous month's total.
    # If prev_month_total_amount is None, month_over_month_change will also be None.
    month_over_month_change=total_payments_for_month - prev_month_total_amount
).ORDER_BY(year_of_payment.ASC(), month_of_payment.ASC())

# Step 1: For each salesperson, define their sales made in the last 30 days
# Also, carry forward first_name and last_name for later use.
salespersons_with_recent_sales_data = Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    sp_first_name=first_name,
    sp_last_name=last_name,
    recent_sales_list=sales_made.WHERE(DATEDIFF("days", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) < 30)
)

# Step 2: Calculate aggregates (count of sales, sum of revenue) from these recent sales.
# Then, select the top 5 salespersons based on the number of recent sales.
top_salespersons_aggregated = salespersons_with_recent_sales_data.CALCULATE(
    sp_first_name=sp_first_name, # Pass through from previous step
    sp_last_name=sp_last_name,   # Pass through from previous step
    num_recent_sales=COUNT(recent_sales_list),
    sum_recent_revenue=SUM(recent_sales_list.sale_price)
).TOP_K(
    5,
    by=num_recent_sales.DESC()
)

# Step 3: Format the final output with the requested field names.
# Ensure total_revenue_amount is 0.0 if there were no sales, rather than null.
top_salespersons_details = top_salespersons_aggregated.CALCULATE(
    first_name=sp_first_name,
    last_name=sp_last_name,
    total_sales_count=num_recent_sales,
    total_revenue_amount=DEFAULT_TO(sum_recent_revenue, 0.0)
)

# First, process customers to gather sales data per state
state_level_sales_data = Customers.WHERE(
    HAS(car_purchases) == 1  # Consider only customers who have made purchases
).PARTITION(
    name="state_groups", by=(state)  # Group customers by their state
).CALCULATE(
    state_name=state,  # The state
    # Sum of sale_price from all purchases made by customers in this state
    total_revenue_for_state=SUM(Customers.car_purchases.sale_price),
    # Count of unique customer IDs in this state group
    num_unique_customers_for_state=NDISTINCT(Customers._id)
)

# Then, select the top 5 states based on total revenue
top_5_states_by_revenue = state_level_sales_data.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_revenue_for_state.DESC()  # Order by total revenue, descending
).CALCULATE(
    state=state_name,  # Output field: state
    num_unique_customers=num_unique_customers_for_state,  # Output field: number of unique customers
    total_revenue=total_revenue_for_state  # Output field: total revenue
)

).PARTITION(

).CALCULATE(

)

# First, process customers to gather sales data per state
state_level_sales_data = Customers.WHERE(
    HAS(car_purchases) == 1  # Consider only customers who have made purchases
).PARTITION(
    name="state_groups", by=(state)  # Group customers by their state
).CALCULATE(
    state_name=state,  # The state
    # Sum of sale_price from all purchases made by customers in this state
    total_revenue_for_state=SUM(Customers.car_purchases.sale_price),
    # Count of unique customer IDs in this state group
    num_unique_customers_for_state=NDISTINCT(Customers._id)
)

# Then, select the top 5 states based on total revenue
top_5_states_by_revenue = state_level_sales_data.TOP_K(
    5, by=total_revenue_for_state.DESC()  # Order by total revenue, descending
).CALCULATE(
    state=state_name,  # Output field: state
    num_unique_customers=num_unique_customers_for_state,  # Output field: number of unique customers
    total_revenue=total_revenue_for_state  # Output field: total revenue
)

# Step 1: Create a collection of sales, augmenting each sale with the make and model of the car sold,
# and the sale price (which we'll call sale_value for clarity in the next step).
sales_details = Sales.CALCULATE(
    car_make_val=car.make,
    car_model_val=car.model,
    sale_value=sale_price
)

# Step 2: Partition these augmented sales records by car make and model.
# For each (make, model) group, calculate the total number of sales and the total revenue.
# Then, select the top 5 groups based on total revenue.
top_selling_car_models = sales_details.PARTITION(
    name="car_model_sales_group", by=(car_make_val, car_model_val)
).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make_val,
    model=car_model_val,
    total_number_of_sales=COUNT(Sales),  # Counts sales records within each (make, model) group
    total_revenue=SUM(Sales.sale_value)  # Sums the sale_value (original sale_price) for each group
).TOP_K(5, by=total_revenue.DESC())

result = top_selling_car_models

# Define the end of the period (inclusive of today's date up to the current time)
end_period_date = DATETIME('now')

# Calculate sales performance for each salesperson within the defined period
sales_performance_data = Dealership.Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    # Store original first and last names with new variable names to avoid conflicts
    sales_rep_fn=first_name,
    sales_rep_ln=last_name,
    # Calculate total revenue from sales made within the period
    # Revenue is the sum of sale_price for sales in the last 3 months
    total_revenue_for_period=SUM(sales_made.WHERE(
        (sale_date >= start_period_date) & (sale_date <= end_period_date)
    ).sale_price),
    # Calculate the total number of sales made within the period
    total_sales_for_period=COUNT(sales_made.WHERE(
        (sale_date >= start_period_date) & (sale_date <= end_period_date)
    ))
)

# From the calculated performance data, filter out salespersons with no sales in the period,
# then select the top 3 based on total revenue, and format the output.
top_sales_representatives = sales_performance_data.WHERE(
    total_sales_for_period > 0  # Ensure the salesperson made at least one sale
).TOP_K(
    3,  # Select the top 3
    by=total_revenue_for_period.DESC()  # Order by total revenue in descending order
).CALCULATE(
    # Select and rename fields for the final output
    first_name=sales_rep_fn,
    last_name=sales_rep_ln,
    total_number_of_sales=total_sales_for_period,
    total_revenue=total_revenue_for_period
)

# Filter payments from the previous ISO week and group by date and payment method
previous_week_payments = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    # Get payments from 14 days ago to 7 days ago (previous ISO week)
    (DATEDIFF("days", payment_date, DATETIME("now")) >= 7) &
    (DATEDIFF("days", payment_date, DATETIME("now")) < 14) &
    # Ensure we're getting the correct ISO week by checking day of week alignment
    (DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) >= DAYOFWEEK(DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-7 days"))) &
    (DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) <= DAYOFWEEK(DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-1 day")))
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date_only=payment_date,
    payment_method_type=payment_method,
    amount=payment_amount
)

# Group by date and payment method to get daily totals
result = previous_week_payments.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups", 
    by=(payment_date_only, payment_method_type)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date_only,
    payment_method=payment_method_type,
    daily_total=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_date.ASC(), payment_method.ASC())

    # Calculate the start of the current ISO week (Monday)
    start_of_current_iso_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    # Calculate the start of the previous ISO week (Monday of last week)
    start_of_previous_iso_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days'),
    # Calculate the end of the previous ISO week (Sunday of last week)
    end_of_previous_iso_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-1 day')
).PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_previous_iso_week) & (payment_date <= end_of_previous_iso_week)
).PARTITION(
